**Self-correction:** in round 3 I labelled the 89469 and 89473 times as "saved". They are the signals' generated-at times; the database rows were created 1.28 s and 1.49 s later (`round04-codex.stderr.txt:2507, 2514`). No conclusion changes.

### R4-1. Codex's rejected-price search is genuine and complete [PE]
- **Coverage:** the archive spans 20:05:00.350Z to 20:59:59.842Z, which covers the whole hold.
- **Result:** zero SHIBUSDT lines matched any of the three rejection formats (`PRICE_NOT_CANONICAL`, `EQUAL_TIMESTAMP_REJECTED`, `outcome=NON_CANONICAL`).
- **The extraction worked:** in the same command it found `positionId=64` 9 times and events 1085348 and 1085485 once each.
- **The patterns cover every rejection path:**
  - Exactly three places in the code count a rejection (`MarketPriceEventService.java:54, 72, 92`).
  - Each one writes a log line that names the symbol (`:56, :70, :94`), at INFO or WARN.
  - The same logger's INFO lines do appear in this log, so those lines would have been written.

### R4-2. Independent cross-checks [PE]
- **Every SHIB price was accepted.** Codex's full SHIB extract for 20:45–20:58Z shows 59 SHIB price observations between the BUY and the SELL (event 1082339 through 1085485). All 59 were accepted (`outcome=APPLIED`).
- **One rejection happened during the hold, for another symbol.** The global rejection counter rose from 68 to 69 once, between 20:52:18Z and 20:53:18Z (23:52–23:53 KSA). Codex's report doesn't mention this, although it is in Codex's own output. The SHIB extract shows only accepted events in that minute, so the rejected price belonged to another symbol.
- **No failures, restart or reconnect:**
  - All failure counters were 0 for the whole process lifetime: input, pipeline, pending-resolved, protection, overflow and submission.
  - The input counter rose steadily, so the application did not restart.
  - Every SHIB kline line shows `generation=2`, so the WebSocket did not reconnect.
  - Only 1m klines feed protection (`BinanceKlineService.java:95, 106-108`).

### R4-3. No accepted price reached TP [PE, with one AI step]
- **The TP branch left no trace.**
  - A price at or above TP enters the TP branch first (`LivePositionProtectionService.java:100`).
  - That branch either extends TP, which writes a `TP_EXTENSION` log line and changes TP in the database, or sells as `TAKE_PROFIT`, which writes a log line and a wallet trade.
  - None of these exist: no log lines (`round04-codex.stderr.txt:5943-5946`), TP unchanged (DBQ-014), and no wallet trade between 106 and 107.
- **Codex's remaining objection (a silent failed sell) is closed [AI from PE].**
  - The sell call returns false only if the position quantity or the coin quantity is zero or less (`WalletAutoExecutionService.java:490-493, 504-505`).
  - Position: `onPrice` already returns early at line 93 if the position quantity is zero or less, within the same transaction.
  - Coins: SELL 107 sold the smaller of position and coin quantity, which equalled BUY 106's full 4,621,072.088724584103 SHIB. Trade IDs 106 and 107 are consecutive, so no trade reduced the coins in between (`DBQ-002:10-11`).
  - The only remaining possibility would be an untracked wallet-balance edit, and there is no evidence of one.
- **The lock path bounds the prices.**
  - The profit-lock evaluator logs whenever the high-water mark, lock or state changes (`DynamicProfitLockService.java:143-150, 168`).
  - It logged nothing between 20:47:41.566Z and 20:58:09.534Z, so no accepted price exceeded 0.00000542 in that period.
- **So protection only ever accepted:** 0.00000541 → 0.00000542 → 0.00000543 → 0.00000542.

### Answers

**Q1. Why did it exit on profit lock instead of take-profit?** [PE]
- **TP was never reached.** TP 0.00000543373 is entry plus 3 × the 1m ATR of 0.00000000791 (`DBQ-007:6`). On the 0.00000001 price grid, reaching it needed 0.00000544, and protection never received that (R4-1 to R4-3).
- **The lock armed and then triggered.**
  - The 0.00000543 tick was 84.2815% of the way to TP.
  - That passed the 70% activation threshold and completed one 10% trail step, so the lock was set at 50% of the TP distance: 0.000005421865 (`ProfitLockPolicy.java:39-49`).
  - The next different price, 0.00000542, was at or below the lock and above the 0.000005412705 hard floor, so the exit policy ran (`LivePositionProtectionService.java:207-219`).
- **The exit policy saw:**
  - 1m WATCH (89795, 42 s old);
  - 5m WATCH (89724, 3 m 39 s old);
  - 1h NEUTRAL (88327, the newest closed 1h candle, 58 m 37 s old).
- **Neither hold rule matched.** A hold needs either a 1h BUY/STRONG_BUY, or a 1h WATCH; both also need a supportive 5m (`PositionExitPolicy.java:58, 67-69`). A NEUTRAL 1h fails both, so the default `PROFIT_LOCK_EXIT` applies (`:88`). Trade 107's stored message records exactly this (`DBQ-002:11`).
- **No delay.** 94 ms passed from observation to decision and 105 ms to the SELL. The inputs were fresh.
- **Classification:** a valid exit that followed the rules. It was not a defect, stale data, an execution delay or a configuration problem.

**Q2. Why was there no continuation around 00:38 KSA?** [PE]
- Continuation is evaluated only in the live TP branch (`LivePositionProtectionService.java:100-107`), and that branch was never entered. The signal-path TP exit (`PaperTradingService.java:170-174`) never calls continuation at all.
- The position closed at 23:58:43 KSA, 39 min 16 s before the 00:38 KSA candle, so nothing was open to extend.
- "12:38" means 00:38 KSA. The investigation was already running at 00:54 KSA on 10 Oct (`DATABASE_QUERY_LOG.md:6`), so 12:38 PM had not yet happened.

**Q3. Why didn't protection re-check the remaining upside?** [PE]
- **The live rule reads three labels, once.** The lock-breach rule reads three decision labels once (`LivePositionProtectionService.java:219`). It does not look at the distance to TP, momentum, or the advisory analysis.
- **The advisory said HOLD, but it can't act.**
  - The position-management analysis 40 s earlier said HOLD, exit score 0/25, `advisoryOnly=true` (log line 45271).
  - Advisory output can only execute a stop-loss (`PositionManagementService.java:125, 130-133`).
  - Live protection doesn't depend on that service at all (`LivePositionProtectionService.java:39-48`).
- **The signal path reads no labels at all.** Its profit-lock exit reads no signal labels before selling (`PaperTradingService.java:176-180`).
- **[AI from PE] Grid geometry left one tick of room.** TP sat 2.373 ticks above entry. The lock armed on the first +2-tick price and sat 0.81 tick below the high, so any one-tick pullback breached it.
- **[AI from PE] The two rules treat the same 1h label differently.**
  - At TP, continuation accepts a NEUTRAL 1h (`PositionContinuationPolicy.java:47, 72-73`).
  - At a lock breach, a hold needs 1h WATCH or better.
  - With these labels, touching TP might have extended the target; that would depend on the 1m trend and momentum scores, which aren't in the bundle. A breach had to sell.
  - This is a design observation, not a defect claim.
- **[AI] Counterfactual:** if the live path had held, signal 89815 (price 0.00000542) would most likely have closed the position through the signal path about 19 s later, at the same price.
- The later rise is outcome only; the exit could not have known it.

## OBJECTIONS
All objections to Codex's round 4 are non-material:

- **O1.** Codex's own output shows the rejection counter going from 68 to 69 during the hold, but its report never accounts for it. I resolved it in R4-2: it wasn't SHIB.
- **O2.** Codex says the "silent failed sell" path can't be proven impossible. It is excluded by the evidence in R4-3; only an untracked wallet-balance edit remains as a theoretical residual.
- **O3.** "Lock armed at 20:58:09.444Z" is the Binance observation time. The database activation time is 20:58:09.534172Z (`DBQ-014:6`); both should be reported. Separately, "below TP … but below the lock" should read "and below the lock".
- **O4.** The "if 12:38 meant noon" hedge is unnecessary (see Q2).
- **O5. Evidence handling is still open, as a process issue only.**
  - Query IDs DBQ-004/005/006 were reused and their first results overwritten (`DATABASE_QUERY_LOG.md:62-117` vs `:271-326`).
  - The query log's stated policy, "raw MySQL timestamps are UTC" (`:22`), doesn't describe these result files, which display KSA. For example, trade 106 shows 23:45:05.997 next to `priceObservedAt=20:45:03.456Z` (`DBQ-002:10`).
  - None of this round's conclusions depend on the overwritten files.
- **O6. [AI] Statistics.** One trade with a later rise proves nothing about a better rule. Any hold or continuation change needs a multi-symbol, out-of-sample test, net of fees and slippage. That test must run in a Replay that first models the signal-path exits.

None of these block approval.

2. **No Replay was run, since it would create Replay state, so I make no parity claim.**
   - Exact-price Replay skips the signal-path exits.
   - Fallback Replay adds continuation and lock-hold logic that the Production signal path doesn't have (`ShadowProductionReplayService.java:183-186, 570-591, 639-645`).
3. **The running code is not proven by a binary hash.**
   - There is no deployed-binary hash; the current source is corroborated only by matching log formats.
   - The 15 s stale and 2 s future limits are code defaults; I didn't check for runtime overrides. That is moot, because nothing was rejected.
4. **Entry points outside Q1–Q3, not assessed:**
   - The 5m wakeup route bought 1.1 s after 89469's own processing declined.
   - 89469's stored risk/reward was 0.877: stop 0.5% below entry, TP 0.44% above (`DBQ-007:6`).
5. **Fees:** automatic trades record a fee of 0. At Binance's regular 0.100% per side [ER], the +0.0462 USDT (+0.185%) gross gain becomes roughly −0.004 USDT net [AI].

## FINDINGS

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** This is the earlier SHIBUSDT position: BUY wallet trade 106 / signal 89469, then SELL 107 / managed position 64. It is not the later 90192/65 trade. Entry: `2026-10-09T20:45:05.997Z` / `23:45:05.997 KSA`; exit: `2026-10-09T20:58:43.554Z` / `23:58:43.554 KSA`.

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE — Q1:** It exited through the live-price `PROFIT_LOCK_EXIT` path because the triggering accepted observation was `0.000005420000`, below TP `0.000005433730` but below the active profit lock `0.000005421865`. The lock had armed at `2026-10-09T20:58:09.444Z` / `23:58:09.444 KSA` when price reached `0.000005430000`, 84.2815% of the entry-to-TP distance.

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** At the lock breach, the current 1m signal was `WATCH` 89795, 5m was `WATCH` 89724, and 1h was `NEUTRAL` 88327. Neither protected-hold condition accepts a neutral 1h, so the default implemented result is `PROFIT_LOCK_EXIT`.

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE — stale/rejected-price challenge resolved:** The original runtime archive covers `20:05–20:59Z`. It contains zero SHIBUSDT `PRICE_NOT_CANONICAL`, `EQUAL_TIMESTAMP_REJECTED`, or `outcome=NON_CANONICAL` entries. Therefore, there is no evidence that a received TP-level observation was rejected as stale, future, equal-timestamp, or out-of-order. Event 1085348 armed the lock and event 1085485 triggered the exit; both committed as `APPLIED`.

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE — Q2:** Continuation is only evaluated in the live TP branch. This position never reached that branch in the evidenced protection path, and it was closed roughly 39 minutes before the preserved `00:38 KSA` candle on 10 October (`2026-10-09T21:38:00Z` / `2026-10-10T00:38:00 KSA`). Thus no position existed to extend then.

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE — Q3:** At a lock breach, protection does not evaluate “remaining upside.” It evaluates the latest three decision labels once and executes the exit policy. It did re-evaluate lock arithmetic at four `0.000005430000` observations before the breach, but continuation is not part of that lock-breach branch. The later rise is outcome-only information.

## OBJECTIONS

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** “TP was never reached” is too broad. The evidence supports only: no system-accepted or system-rejected TP-level observation is recorded. Because protection receives kline `c`, it cannot establish that Binance never traded at TP between received updates.

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** A TP-branch execution failure can return `false` without a dedicated TP-failure log if no sellable wallet quantity exists: [WalletAutoExecutionService.java](/C:/workspace/crypto-ai-trader-mysql/src/main/java/com/crypto/wallet/service/WalletAutoExecutionService.java:490). No evidence indicates that occurred; 34 seconds later the profit-lock path sold the full position. The preserved bundle nevertheless lacks a wallet-asset history sufficient to prove that theoretical path impossible.

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** Replay cannot support a claim that holding would improve this trade. Exact Replay skips signal-price mechanics, while fallback Replay applies continuation/lock-hold logic differently: [ShadowProductionReplayService.java](/C:/workspace/crypto-ai-trader-mysql/src/main/java/com/crypto/regression/service/ShadowProductionReplayService.java:183), [line 562](/C:/workspace/crypto-ai-trader-mysql/src/main/java/com/crypto/regression/service/ShadowProductionReplayService.java:562).

- **AGENT INFERENCE:** One subsequent upswing is not evidence for an improved exit rule. Any proposed hold/continuation change requires out-of-sample, fee/slippage-aware evaluation after Replay/Production exit parity is established.

## UNKNOWNS

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** Whether Binance printed TP intraminute between received `c` observations remains unknown; it was not information this protection design necessarily received.

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** No immutable deployed-binary hash is preserved. The current source is corroborated by matching runtime log formats and recorded behavior, not independently proven as the exact runtime artifact.

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** The preserved evidence identifies `00:38 KSA` on 10 October. If “12:38 KSA” meant noon, it is outside this evidence window.

This file contains permanent project instructions.

or approves the modification.

After every implementation, Codex MUST determine whether the change affects
architecture, trading behavior, operational behavior, or previous technical
decisions.

### Architecture changes

- data flow

not only what was originally planned.

## Definition of Done

A task is NOT COMPLETE until all required documentation has been updated.

Before finishing a task, Codex MUST explicitly check:
"Does this change require documentation updates?"

Do not wait for the user to ask for documentation updates.


## Trade investigations
When asked to investigate a trade:

- Confirm the application, environment, symbol, and trade ID.
- Use configured database and SSH connections without displaying secrets.
- Use read-only, bounded queries and targeted log searches.
- Read the actual schema before choosing tables or columns.
- Reconstruct the timeline: candle, signal, decision, entry, protection,
  and exit. Display timestamps in KSA (UTC+03:00), interpreting stored
  timestamps as UTC.
- Trace the relevant code and configuration for each decision.
- Check data freshness, entry quality, strategy, risk/reward, fees,
  stop-loss, and exit reason where applicable.
- Assess the entry using only information available at that moment.
  Use subsequent price action separately to evaluate the outcome.
- Distinguish a valid losing trade from a logic defect, stale data,
  execution delay, configuration issue, or missing evidence.
- Check whether the behavior can be reproduced in Replay.
- Support findings with record IDs, log timestamps, and code references.
- State uncertainties; do not invent a cause.
- Do not change code, configuration, services, or production data during
  an investigation unless the user requests a fix.

Report:
1. What happened.
2. Why the system entered and exited.
3. Whether the behavior followed the intended rules.
4. Confirmed defects and missing evidence.
5. Recommended correction and regression checks, if needed.

## Current architecture direction (pending implementation)

The current priority is to remove the trader's runtime dependency on the
collector. This is a design decision, not an implemented/deployed change.

- Target runtime: trader-owned Binance kline ingestion and local
  `crypto_ai.candle` writes with `shared-market.mode=OFF`.
- The collector remains independent in `crypto_ai_v2`; the trader must not
  connect to, read from, or write collector stream/cursor tables at runtime.
- Do not run direct ingestion and the shared-market executors together.
- Applied shared-market tables and Flyway history are historical evidence; do
  not drop, reset, or edit them merely to enable the new path.
- `V103__fix_149_fresh_admission_indexes` still requires
  `crypto_ai_v2.market_data_stream_event` for a fresh Flyway install. Treat
  trader-only bootstrap as a release blocker until a forward, tested installer
  policy exists. Never edit an applied Flyway migration.

## Local-candle implementation safety

When implementing or reviewing the local-candle design:

- Keep `shared-market.fix142.enabled=false`,
  `shared-market.fix142.acknowledgment-enabled=false`, and scheduled analysis
  disabled for the OFF-mode cutover unless a reviewed change explicitly says
  otherwise.
- Preserve the input -> protection -> observer -> close-dispatch transaction
  order. Candle close analysis must not run on the Binance ingestion thread.
- A closed candle needs durable/recoverable analysis admission; an in-memory
  event/queue alone loses work across a crash. Recovery must distinguish fresh
  execution-capable work from historical-only analysis.
- Candle updates must be monotonic: a late open update cannot reopen or
  overwrite a closed candle. Publish close work only for an atomic
  open-to-closed transition.
- A WebSocket reconnect can occur at a candle-close boundary and Binance may
  not replay that final closed-kline event. Candle recovery is automatic: use
  an exact authoritative Binance REST candle only after exchange-time
  finality and the measured live-close grace. It may either finalise a matching
  existing `closed=0` row or insert a wholly absent identity inside the active
  data-quality window. A recovered open row preserves provenance; an inserted
  gap row uses `BACKFILL` provenance. Neither path may alter a completed row,
  create a close-transition token, dispatch analysis, create a live signal,
  or execute a trade. The recovery must have durable retry/audit evidence,
  bounded rate-limit handling, health visibility, and no manual repair step.
- Automation direction: no pending candle or analysis outcome may require
  manual approval, manual database repair, or a persisted trading fence.
  Reconciliation must retry and resolve automatically, or age out to a durable
  explicit outcome with a visible reason. This does not permit unverified data
  to masquerade as valid or force an entry: a genuine strategy/risk veto or
  unavailable authoritative data remains non-executable evidence, not a
  silently stuck pending signal.
- Gap backfill must be a pure insert-if-absent operation. It must never use a
  duplicate-key update that could finalise or alter a live row which appeared
  while the authoritative REST request was in progress. Recovery candidates
  are bounded by the active quality window and terminal audit outcomes are not
  retried. Each candle/work path must write one durable terminal analysis
  outcome; transient failures remain retryable and write no terminal outcome.
- Any future universal trading-mutation barrier is an in-memory drain
  participant only. Outside an active drain it must not block, delay, queue,
  or persistently fence fresh trading, signals, or protection. It may reject
  new mutations only while draining and must then wait for admitted mutations
  to finish before shutdown.
- Historical-only recovery must be structurally unable to reach execution,
  wallet, position, order, or executable-work services. Quarantined work with
  possible financial effects may be concluded automatically only from proven
  durable effect evidence (`EFFECT_COMMITTED` or `EFFECT_ABSENT`); unknown
  atomicity remains a release blocker. Binance REST rate-limit/ban handling
  must be shared by reconciliation, clock sync, bootstrap, and other REST
  callers.
- Enforce freshness from persisted candle close time before analysis and again
  before execution. Historical-only signals must be explicitly marked and
  excluded from live-latency monitoring.
- Price protection must receive the Binance observation timestamp and validate
  age/order inside the protection transaction. Paper-trading effects and their
  accepted price-event state must commit atomically; any future live-exchange
  effect needs a separate idempotent design.
- Do not add manual approval, persisted review fences, or database locks that
  block new analysis, signals, entries, or protection unless the user changes
  that direction. In-process coordination and normal database constraints are
  distinct from persisted trading fences.
- OFF-mode health/admin endpoints must not query or write retired shared-market
  tables. Existing `REVIEW_REQUIRED` history must not block OFF-mode work.
- Keep production and Replay evidence aligned. Replay must fail a parity claim
  for unresolved direct price-event evidence, while deliberate exclusions stay
  excluded.

## Delivery discipline

- Treat the local-candle architecture as not deployment-ready until its
  migration/bootstrap policy, concurrency, crash/restart, stale-input,
  Replay-parity, and Java 21/MySQL validation gates pass.
- Do not deploy or change production configuration/data unless the user
  explicitly requests it.
- Protection coverage for open positions during application replacement remains
  an unresolved release gate. Do not represent the local-candle design as
  deployment-ready until it is implemented or conclusively disproven.

## FIX-154: pending higher-timeframe BUY validation

FIX-154 is a separate, not-yet-implemented design. Do not merge it into the
local-candle change or describe it as deployed.

Purpose: a historical bearish 1h signal may block or delay a current 1m BUY
only when its own evidence is fresh, data-quality-valid, and sufficiently
strong. A historical `btcContextEntryAllowed=false` must remain investigatory
evidence, not a higher-timeframe validity gate; the current 1m signal's own
BTC protection remains unchanged.

- Score usable bearish 1h evidence into `NOT_TRUSTED`, `SOFT_CONFLICT`, or
  `HARD_BEARISH_CANDIDATE` using closed-candle local indicators.
- A hard veto requires strong 1h evidence plus fresh, quality-valid bearish
  5m confirmation. Stale, invalid, or unknown historical quality can never
  hard-veto.
- Persist `trade_signal.data_quality_valid` from
  `MarketContextSnapshot.dataValid()` for every new signal. Historical NULL
  means unknown/invalid; never backfill it to true.
- A confirmed 1h recovery may release only a soft conflict and only with fresh,
  quality-valid, non-bearish 5m evidence.
- Preserve the legacy `5M_BEARISH_VETO` behavior: the new data-quality gate
  applies to the new hard-veto/recovery paths, not that legacy fallback.
- Add immutable BUY-evaluation audit evidence; maintain Replay parity and
  prevent future-candle or wall-clock look-ahead.

# MANDATORY PRE-IMPLEMENTATION CHANGE CONTRACT

This section extends the existing governance. It does not replace any earlier
project, investigation, architecture, trading, safety, or documentation rule.

For any material code change, implementation is forbidden until the team has
produced and approved the same exact revision of:

`agent-docs/FINAL_CHANGE_SPECIFICATION.md`

The specification is the implementation contract and MUST contain, at minimum:

1. Business objective and the exact agreed business line/flow.
2. Confirmed root cause and evidence.
3. In-scope and out-of-scope boundaries.
4. Complete class/file change registry (modify/create/delete/rename/config/
   migration/test/documentation).
5. Class-by-class and material method-by-method current behavior -> agreed new
   behavior, reason, inputs/outputs, side effects, error handling, concurrency,
   transactionality, Replay impact and Production impact.
6. Mandatory logging registry covering all materially affected areas. Logs must
   make the business flow and decisions reconstructable without exposing
   credentials/secrets. Include relevant correlation/trade/symbol/interval/
   candle/test identifiers and respect the project's UTC/KSA rules.
7. Database, configuration and trading-behavior registries, explicitly stating
   NONE when there is no impact.
8. Test and validation registry, including positive, negative, regression,
   failure/recovery, concurrency where relevant, Replay, and Production-safe
   validation.
9. Risks, rollback plan, observability plan and documentation registry.
10. Master implementation registry assigning an ID to every authorized change,
    log, DB/config item, test and documentation update.

No source/config/database/trading change may be implemented unless it is
registered in the approved specification. No unrelated refactoring or cleanup
may be hidden inside an approved fix.

## Dual-agent specification approval

The SAME exact specification revision must be fully reviewed and explicitly
approved by BOTH agents:

- Codex: APPROVED
- Claude: APPROVED
- Unresolved material disagreements: 0

There is no majority vote and no fixed review-iteration limit. Any material
revision invalidates both prior approvals and requires a complete re-review by
both agents. Even after dual approval, implementation remains NOT AUTHORIZED
until the user explicitly approves that exact revision.

## Code implementation direction

After explicit user approval:

- Codex is the implementation owner.
- Claude is the mandatory independent code reviewer.
- Claude reviews the COMPLETE implementation, not only the latest patch.
- Claude comments/rejects; Codex corrects or disputes with technical evidence;
  Claude re-reviews the complete updated implementation.
- Repeat for as many iterations as required. There is no fixed iteration cap.
- Codex cannot self-approve its implementation and the Coordinator cannot
  overrule a material Claude objection.
- Any implementation-code change after Claude approval invalidates that
  approval and requires another complete Claude review.
- A material deviation from the approved specification stops implementation,
  reopens dual-agent design review, creates a new specification revision, and
  requires renewed user approval.

## Post-implementation registry reconciliation

Before technical acceptance, reconcile the actual implementation against the
approved master registry. Every planned item must be accounted for and every
actual change must map to an approved registry ID.

Acceptance requires at minimum:

- missing registered changes: NONE
- unregistered implementation changes: NONE
- unresolved material review findings: 0
- required logging implemented and reviewed
- required tests/validation completed with evidence
- Replay/Production parity reviewed where applicable
- required documentation updates completed
- Claude final full-code review: APPROVED
- Codex final verification of the exact Claude-approved state: PASSED

===== codex-docs/ARCHITECTURE.md =====
# Crypto AI Trader Г?" Architecture

Status labels used in this document:

- **Current** Г?" present in the repository today.
- **Pending** Г?" approved design direction, not implemented or deployed.
- **Legacy/shared** Г?" existing shared-market path retained as historical evidence
  while the trader moves to local-candle ownership.

## Current release status

**FIX-153 is implemented in source but is NOT APPROVED FOR PRODUCTION.**
It moves the trader toward collector-free, trader-owned local candle ingestion
in `shared-market.mode=OFF`. Java 21, disposable-MySQL, crash/race, Windows
two-process, soak, OFF-mode and Replay-parity proof gates remain open.

FIX-154 is a separate pending higher-timeframe design. It is not implemented
and is not part of FIX-153 deployment scope.

## System purpose

The application is a Spring Boot cryptocurrency trader that analyses Binance
market data, persists candles and technical indicators, creates trade signals,
and performs paper-wallet execution and position protection. Production and
Replay are intended to use equivalent persisted market evidence.

## Runtime and data ownership

| Area | Current ownership | Target direction |
|---|---|---|
| Trader database | `crypto_ai` | Remains the trader's only runtime database. |
| Collector database | `crypto_ai_v2` | Collector remains separate and is not read/written by the trader at runtime. |
| Candle ingestion | In OFF mode, trader-owned Binance ingestion writes local `crypto_ai.candle`. | Must be proven in a collector-free deployment rehearsal. |
| Candle processing | Bounded lanes, close-transition tokens and a local detector are implemented. | MySQL/race/crash proof remains required. |
| Trading | Paper wallet and managed positions. | Live-exchange orders are outside the local-candle automatic-resolution design. |

## Current primary flow

```text
Binance WebSocket kline
  -> BinanceKlineService
  -> KlineTransactionCoordinator
      -> input transaction: candle + canonical 1m market price event
      -> protection transaction: LivePositionProtectionService
      -> observer transaction
      -> close-dispatch transaction
  -> CandleClosedEvent (AFTER_COMMIT)
  -> CandleAnalysisDispatcher
  -> CandleClosedAnalysisWorker
  -> TechnicalIndicatorService
  -> AnalysisService / TradeSignal
  -> SignalProcessingCoordinator
  -> PaperTradingService / wallet and managed-position state
```

`KlineTransactionCoordinator` preserves input -> protection -> observer ->
close-dispatch ordering. Closed-candle analysis is asynchronous and must not
run on the Binance ingestion thread.

## Current core persistence

| Table/domain | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `candle` | Local OHLCV candle history keyed by symbol, interval, and open time. |
| `technical_indicator` | Persisted indicator snapshots derived from closed candles. |
| `trade_signal` | Persisted analysis decisions and their context. |
| `signal_processing_work` | Durable processing registration/status for a persisted signal. |
| `market_price_event` | Canonical 1m price evidence used by protection and Replay. |
| Paper wallet / position tables | Paper executions, open-position state, TP/SL/profit protection. |

## Legacy/shared-market path

**Current but pending retirement from the trader runtime.**

The shared path includes `SharedMarketSource`, `SharedMarketConsumer`,
`SharedAnalysisDispatcher`, cutover/readiness logic, and local historical
tables such as `shared_market_event_delivery`, `shared_market_consumer_state`,
and `shared_market_health`. These records remain evidence and must not be
dropped, reset, or used to manufacture an approval.

The collector event/cursor source is `crypto_ai_v2.market_data_stream_event`
and `market_data_stream_cursor`. In the target local-candle runtime, the
trader must not connect to, read, or write these collector tables.

`V103__fix_149_fresh_admission_indexes` is an existing Java Flyway migration
that requires the collector event table for a fresh installation. A trader-only
bootstrap policy is therefore **pending** and remains a release blocker. Do
not edit applied Flyway migrations or Flyway history.

## Implemented, unverified: local-candle architecture (FIX-153)

The implemented intended mode is `shared-market.mode=OFF` with trader-owned Binance kline
ingestion and local candle writes. The shared executors and direct-ingestion
path must never run together.

Implemented safeguards:

- Candle writes are monotonic: a late open update cannot reopen or overwrite a
  closed candle.
- A closed-candle transition is admitted exactly once using an atomic upsert
  result plus in-process admission coordination.
- A bounded local detector recovers a closed candle that was committed before
  the in-memory event/dispatcher could start analysis.
- Fresh candles may execute; stale candles are analysed historically only, or
  their already-owned work is expired without business execution.
- Analysis lanes are bounded. Under overload, 1m may retain only the latest
  pending candle; higher intervals use an explicit bounded FIFO policy.
- Historic shared `REVIEW_REQUIRED` evidence must not block OFF-mode work.
- OFF-mode health/admin endpoints must use local-candle health and must not
  query retired shared-market tables.
- Current REST backfill can finalise an interrupted open row but cannot mint a
  runtime close token or dispatch analysis. Runtime close tokens plus
  provenance keep backfill out of detector admission. The approved, pending
  automatic-reconciliation extension will also insert an exact wholly absent
  candle only inside the active quality window, with `BACKFILL` provenance and
  the same no-token/no-dispatch boundary.
- Observed local behavior (2026-10-09): a WebSocket reconnect at a candle-close
  boundary can leave the already-persisted 1m/5m rows open because Binance does
  not replay the final closed-kline event. The current detector recovers missing
  analysis admission, not missing close facts. An authoritative, bounded REST
  repair may therefore finalise the exact `closed=0` row or, under the approved
  gap-backfill scope, insert an exact absent identity. It must preserve existing
  provenance, use `BACKFILL` for an insertion, create no close-transition token,
  and never dispatch historical work. Automatic reconciliation of this reconnect
  case remains a FIX-153 release gate; the one-time local repair is evidence,
  not proof that the gap is closed.
- Approved pending reconciliation design: gap backfill is insert-only (no
  duplicate-key update); it can never alter a live row that appears during a
  REST request. Candidate selection is bounded to the active quality window;
  terminal audit records are not retried. Every candle/work route must produce
  a durable terminal outcome or remain explicitly retryable after a transient
  failure. Historical-only processing has no execution authority.
- The pending universal mutation barrier is an in-memory drain participant,
  not a database or persistent trading fence. Outside drain it must not delay,
  queue, or block fresh trading, signals, or protection. Open-position
  protection during application replacement remains an unresolved release gate.
- Local source now contains the first reconciliation implementation slice:
  V107 durable audit/outcome tables, update-only authoritative open-row repair,
  pure insert-only gap backfill, a dedicated recovery executor, a reconnect
  request hook, and drain participation. It is disabled by default and remains
  unvalidated on MySQL; it has no deployment approval.
- Direct prices are normalised to `DECIMAL(30,12)` before persistence,
  protection and completion. Equal timestamps have one canonical receipt.
- Every processing attempt is freshness-checked after normal locks using the
  database-to-exchange correction. JVM-origin timestamp decisions use the
  separately measured JVM-to-exchange correction. The measurement performs no
  HTTP under execution locks and uses a bounded five-second time-only client.
  General Binance REST calls use the configured request timeout, and scheduled
  clock sampling has a four-thread Spring scheduler pool.
  Bootstrap obtains time/klines before opening its local candle-write
  transaction, so a stalled REST request does not retain database resources.
  REST bootstrap gets Binance server time before klines.
- An ADMIN-only drain stops analysis admission, exits the JVM on successful
  quiesce, and resumes admission on timeout. Price protection pauses during
  the controlled drain window.

## Implemented, unverified: protection price-event state machine

For direct 1m price observations, the target evidence lifecycle is:

```text
PENDING
  -> APPLIED                 (protection effects and state commit together)
  -> NON_CANONICAL           (invalid, stale, out-of-order, or duplicate)
  -> PROTECTION_NOT_APPLIED  (confirmed rollback/crash resolution)
```

Replay and price authority consume only accepted `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` rows.
Replay must reject a parity claim when the requested range contains unresolved
direct `PENDING` evidence; this must not block live trading.

The automatic resolver is valid only for the paper-trading phase, where every
protection effect commits in the same transaction as its `APPLIED` update.
Before deployment, the code must prove that there are no asynchronous,
`REQUIRES_NEW`, in-memory-only, or external effects in this boundary. A future
live-exchange order needs a separate idempotent/exchange-reconciliation design.

The target runtime also requires exactly one active trader process. This is an
operational prerequisite, not a database lease or fence.

## Pending: FIX-154 higher-timeframe BUY validation

FIX-154 is separate from the local-candle change and is not implemented.

Its purpose is to let a historical bearish 1h signal affect a current 1m BUY
only when the historical evidence is fresh, quality-valid, and sufficiently
strong. `btcContextEntryAllowed=false` on a historical signal is investigatory
evidence, not a higher-timeframe quality gate; the current 1m BTC protection
remains unchanged.

The design includes reliability bands (`NOT_TRUSTED`, `SOFT_CONFLICT`,
`HARD_BEARISH_CANDIDATE`), required fresh/quality-valid bearish 5m support for
a hard veto, persisted `trade_signal.data_quality_valid`, recovery release
rules, immutable outcome audit evidence, and Replay/no-look-ahead parity.

The implementation design is maintained in `codex-docs/FIX-154_TECHNICAL_DESIGN.md`.
It supersedes no existing deployed behavior and is deliberately separate from
the unrelated legacy readiness document under `src/main/resources/md/FIX-146.md`.

## Deployment posture

Neither FIX-153 nor FIX-154 is deployment-ready. FIX-153 source hardening is
implemented but unverified; FIX-154 remains pending.

Required validation includes Java 21 build/tests, disposable MySQL bootstrap,
real-MySQL concurrency and lock-wait tests, crash/restart tests, stale-input
tests, Replay parity checks, OFF-mode health/admin checks, collector-free
runtime verification, deterministic dispatcher/detector/drain tests,
two-process Windows lock rehearsal, and sustained-load observation across
enabled symbols and intervals. This local workspace has Java 17 and cannot
compile the Java 21 project.

No production configuration, database, service, or deployment change is made
without explicit user approval.

## FIX-153 validation status (2026-10-09)

The reconciliation component now has deterministic Java 21 coverage for its
core admission and outcome contract. The tests execute the real audit and
outcome stores against H2 while mocking only Binance and the candle write
boundary. They prove that healthy delayed live closes are not taken over and
that recovery remains token-free/non-live when it is positively evidenced.

The real-MySQL suite contains matching update-only and insert-only assertions,
but it requires a deliberately disposable loopback MySQL instance and is not
run by the ordinary unit-test goal. This is a release gate, not a warning that
can be waived. The shared Binance 429/418 admission gate, universal mutation
barrier, structural historical-only isolation, financial-effect reconciler,
atomicity/crash proofs, replacement-time position protection, Replay parity,
and soak validation remain incomplete. Therefore `local-candle.reconciliation.enabled`
must stay `false`.

The single `local-candle` configuration mapping is a startup invariant. It now
contains both reconciliation and existing local-runtime properties; a second
mapping is invalid YAML and prevents the Spring application context from
starting.

### Shared Binance REST admission

`BinanceRestAdmissionGate` owns one process-wide REST cooldown. It is consulted
immediately before every call through `BinanceMarketDataClient`; a 429/418
response extends the cooldown using `Retry-After` when it is supplied, otherwise
the bounded fallback. This protects all local REST consumers from repeatedly
calling Binance during a rate-limit/ban response. It is deliberately not part
of the WebSocket or protection path and is not a persisted trading fence.

### Outcome-schema startup invariant

The active signal-processing runtime writes `candle_analysis_outcome` for
terminal analysis evidence. V108 therefore ships with the FIX-154 release even
though local-candle reconciliation remains disabled. `CandleAnalysisOutcomeSchemaVerifier`
runs after Flyway and fails application startup with a clear message if the
table is absent; this prevents later processing transactions from failing only
when they attempt their first outcome write.
# FIX-154 implementation update Г?" 2026-10-10

`HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentService` is the single pure assessment boundary for
the 5m/1h BUY context. It returns effective decisions separately from the JPA
entities, so a SOFT 1h conflict can be assessed as effective `NEUTRAL` without
ever changing the persisted signal. `TradeExecutionValidationService` uses it
for direct BUY and accumulated-evidence authority; `OpportunityConsolidationService`
uses the same assessment for consolidation. A trusted SOFT bearish 1h limits an
initial entry to 25%; a trusted HARD or unknown bearish 1h rejects it, while
the legacy 5m bearish veto keeps priority.

`buy_htf_assessment_audit` is append-only operational evidence. Its writer is
best-effort and uses a bounded dedicated executor: auditing never borrows an
execution transaction's database connection, is skipped in Replay, and cannot
be part of a BUY decision. The progressive add route retains its
existing raw-1h guard; it writes `SOFT_HTF_ADD_BLOCKED` evidence only after
that guard has already rejected an add.

`GET /api/fix154/health` exposes non-gating counters for untrusted bearish
blocks, soft caps, soft add blocks, and best-effort audit failures.

## FIX-153 automatic local-candle reconciliation Г?" enabled 2026-10-10

In `shared-market.mode=OFF`, `LocalCandleReconciliationService` runs every
five seconds on its dedicated single-worker executor. It finds eligible
missing or interrupted candle identities inside the data-quality window, reads
the authoritative Binance candle, and writes only verified final evidence.
An existing open row is finalized by an update-only path; a missing row uses a
plain insert-if-absent backfill path. Neither route creates a close token,
dispatches analysis, or executes a trade. The next genuine live close resumes
ordinary analysis after the quality window is complete.

Twelve symbols had one missing 1m candle: the interval from `2026-10-08T22:18:00Z` to `2026-10-08T22:19:00Z` (KSA `01:18` to `01:19`). This coincides with the local application startup period; the check establishes the gap but does not prove its cause.

# Signal-staleness root cause Г?" 2026-10-09

- System Health CRITICAL signal-staleness alerts were confirmed as accurate for 1m and 5m analysis, not a dashboard timezone issue. At the check, candles continued through `2026-10-09T06:43:00Z` (1m) and `06:35:00Z` (5m), while the newest live signals were `04:34:03Z` (1m) and `04:30:07Z` (5m).
- The most recent 210-candle data-quality window has a synchronized gap for every enabled symbol: missing 1m open `2026-10-09T04:34:00Z` (KSA 07:34) and missing 5m open `2026-10-09T04:30:00Z` (KSA 07:30).
- Cause confirmed from the WebSocket lifecycle log: generation 1 was revoked at `2026-10-09T04:35:00.133951100Z`; generation 2 was admitted at `04:35:01.369103400Z` (1,236 ms interruption). The affected 1m and 5m candles had both closed at the reconnect boundary. Binance did not replay their final closed-kline events, leaving the already-created local rows open rather than finalized.
- `CandleDataQualityService` is configured with `minimum-candles: 210` and `maximum-missing-candles: 0`. Therefore one missing candle makes the entire relevant analysis window invalid. Runtime logs confirm `DATA_QUALITY_BLOCKED` with `Missing 1 candle(s) in the analysis window` for current 1m and 5m worker attempts.
- 1h has no comparable gap and produced a live signal at `2026-10-09T06:00:03Z` (KSA 09:00).
- No repair or configuration change was made. Correct remediation must restore authoritative missing candles rather than relaxing the zero-missing data-quality safety rule.

## Bounded local repair completed

- Authorized local repair finalized the 42 interrupted `RUNTIME` rows using Binance REST candles for the exact identities above: 21 x 1m and 21 x 5m.
- Each response was validated for the expected open and close timestamp before one transaction finalized only a row where `closed=0`. Existing closed candles, provenance, and close-transition tokens were not changed; no close-dispatch, analysis, signal, or trade was initiated by the repair.
- Verification: all 1m/5m 210-candle quality windows had zero gaps after the repair. The next live close produced new LIVE signals at `2026-10-09T07:30:07Z` for both 1m and 5m.

# ETHUSDT paper-trade 59 investigation Г?" 2026-10-09

- Scope: local `crypto_ai` paper trade `wallet_trade.id=59` for `ETHUSDT`; all database reads were bounded and read-only. Stored UTC timestamps are shown below with their KSA (`UTC+03:00`) interpretation.
- Entry: signal `trade_signal.id=69226` was generated at `2026-10-09T07:33:00.233691Z` (10:33:00.234 KSA) from the 1m candle opened at 10:32 KSA. It was a `BUY`, score 79, confidence 70, entry allowed, at 2502.77. The trade executed at `07:33:01.654548Z` (10:33:01.655 KSA), 1.42 seconds after the signal; this is not an execution-latency issue.
- Exit: `wallet_trade.id=60` closed the position at 2504.30 at `07:34:34.196100Z` (10:34:34.196 KSA) with `PROFIT_LOCK_EXIT`. The managed position recorded a high of 2505.80 and an activated profit-lock price of 2504.429502243252. The realized paper gain was about 0.02445 USDT before fees (0.015982291620 ETH x 1.53 USDT).
- Market path: ETH rose from 2485.88 at 07:54 KSA to 2502.77 at entry (+16.89, +0.68%). It reached 2505.56 at 10:25 KSA before the entry; entry was 2.79 (0.11%) below that high. The post-entry high was 2505.93 at 10:34 KSA, only 3.16 (0.13%) above entry, then the candle retraced. This supports the user's observation that entry occurred late in the observed move.
- Primary cause: the local candle-quality interruption stopped 1m ETH signals after 07:34 KSA. There were no ETH 1m signals from 07:34 to 10:26 KSA, the period containing most of the rise. The interrupted 1m and 5m rows were repaired at about 10:28 KSA, so analysis resumed only near the later consolidation/breakout attempt. This is a confirmed data-availability gap, not proof that the strategy would have bought near 07:54 KSA.
- Immediate signal sequence after recovery: at 10:30 KSA the 5m signal was a BUY but blocked by `RANGE_ENTRY_LOCATION`; at 10:31 KSA the 1m raw BUY was vetoed by a bearish BTC context; at 10:33 KSA BTC context became neutral and the 1m trend-following BUY was allowed. The entry signal itself explicitly recorded mixed higher-timeframe context (5m BUY, 1h NEUTRAL) and a weakening but ultra-close ask wall before its target.
- Assessment: the trade followed the implemented rules once local analysis resumed, and the trade was entered/exited promptly. It was nevertheless a late entry relative to the full 07:54Г?"10:33 KSA advance because the system lacked signal coverage during the main move. The data gap is an operational defect already covered by the local-candle recovery deployment gate. Whether the unobserved earlier period would have produced an executable BUY is uncertain without a controlled Replay run; none was started because that would create replay state.
# SHIBUSDT signal #89469 / position #35 Г?" 2026-10-10

- Scope: read-only local `crypto_ai` investigation of wallet trades `106`/`107`,
  paper position `35`, managed position `64`, and signal `89469`.
- Timestamp correction (2026-10-10): the preceding interpretation was wrong
  because the read-only investigation connection used MySQL's `SYSTEM` timezone
  (KSA), while the application connection explicitly sets its session to UTC.
  For position `35`, MySQL's KSA session renders `opened_at` as
  `2026-10-09 23:45:06.682175`; converting that instant to UTC yields
  `2026-10-09 20:45:06.682175`. The Inspector's main trade card parses backend
  timestamps as UTC and renders them in the browser-local timezone. Therefore,
  with a KSA browser, its displayed 23:45 entry time is correct. Do not add
  three hours to the value shown by this KSA-session SQL connection.
- Signal confirmation: `trade_signal.id=89469` was generated at
  `2026-10-09 23:45:03.498678` KSA (`20:45:03.498678` UTC), from the 1m
  candle opened at 23:44 KSA. It is `WATCH`, score 60, confidence 74,
  `WEAK_UPTREND`, `TREND_FOLLOWING`, and `final_entry_allowed=1`.
- Entry: `0.000005410`; stop `0.000005382950`; TP `0.000005433730`; the
  25%-size setup-wakeup route entered from a 5m BUY with 1h NEUTRAL context.
- Exit: highest recorded price before close was `0.000005430`, or 84.2815% of
  the original TP distance. The dynamic lock activated at `0.000005421865` and
  live price `0.000005420` breached it. At the breach the current 1m and 5m
  were WATCH and 1h was NEUTRAL. The shared exit policy therefore returned
  `PROFIT_LOCK_EXIT`; it requires bullish 1h plus supportive 5m, or WATCH 1h
  plus supportive 5m, to hold a breached lock.
- TP extension: not evaluated. Extension begins only once price is at or above
  the original TP. The recorded high remained below TP at the time of exit.
- Subsequent action: later local candles reached `0.000005500` at about 00:40
  KSA, above the original TP. This is hindsight, not evidence available at the
  exit. Local SHIB 1m/5m coverage ends at about 00:56 KSA, so any claimed
  continuation through 12:15 KSA is not verifiable from local candle evidence.
- Assessment: behavior followed the existing protection rule, without a
  confirmed execution or latency defect. A future exit-policy change could
  explicitly allow a near-TP WATCH/WATCH/NEUTRAL continuation hold under
  measurable momentum/structure conditions and a tighter safety floor; it
  requires a separate design and Replay validation.

# SHIBUSDT #89469 near-TP continuation investigation Г?" 2026-10-10

- Status: investigation approved; implementation is explicitly not approved.
  This workstream must not alter or delay FIX-153 release work.
- Hypothesis to test: a breached profit lock close to target may sometimes
  continue when current market structure and momentum remain supportive.
- Important counter-hypothesis: `1h=NEUTRAL` is not positive continuation
  evidence merely because 1m/5m are `WATCH`. Relaxing that guard on distance
  to TP alone could create false holds in weak or sideways markets.
- Required evidence before a policy change: use historical Replay/production
  evidence to compare the current exit rule against a hypothetical bounded
  hold using only information available at the breach. Segment results by
  1h/5m context, regime, trend structure, momentum, retracement depth and
  target-progress band. Evaluate TP hit rate, retained profit, adverse move,
  drawdown, and false-hold frequency. No post-exit price may inform entry or
  exit eligibility.
- Rejected approach: do not authorize continuation from `WATCH/WATCH/NEUTRAL`
  or near-TP distance alone.
- Code-review refinement: `PositionExitPolicy` creates a sharp tier boundary:
  with supportive 5m/current evidence, 1h `WATCH` can hold a breached lock but
  1h `NEUTRAL` falls through to `PROFIT_LOCK_EXIT`. Existing FIX-11T near-TP
  protection arms at 90% target progress and is evaluated after the ordinary
  lock-breach exit, so it could not affect #89469 at 84.28% progress.
- Fee-model refinement: `WalletAutoExecutionService` writes `fee_usdt=0` for
  paper BUY, SELL, and near-TP partial-harvest executions. Any policy Replay
  comparison must add an explicit fee/slippage model before claiming protected
  gross profit is a net gain.
- Approved experiment protocol from independent review: Production policy is
  unchanged. Compare (A) existing immediate lock exit, (B) a one-window,
  near-TP continuation using existing time-safe structure/momentum/context
  calculations, and (C) a volatility-adjusted tighter trailing exit. Use a
  pre-registered 85% TP-progress primary threshold; #89469 at 84.28% does not
  qualify and must not drive threshold selection. Require non-bearish 5m/1h,
  a fee-adjusted protected floor with modeled adverse fills, explicit numeric
  momentum/retracement definitions, no timer resets or repeated extensions,
  and strictly as-of-breach evidence. Evaluate net expectancy, drawdown,
  false-hold/adverse-move rate, and include later-decline counterexamples;
  reserve an out-of-sample period before any implementation decision.
- Final protocol controls: calculate TP progress only from immutable original
  entry and original TP; exclude and report zero/negative/invalid target
  distance. Every A/B/C candidate must use identical historical data,
  evaluation periods, and fee/slippage assumptions. Candidate B must model an
  intrawindow price path sufficiently to determine which of its emergency
  floor or target was reached first; candle high/low alone is not evidence of
  that order. Pre-register the minimum sample size, required net-expectancy
  improvement, and maximum allowed drawdown deterioration before results are
  viewed, then report the qualification count for the B cohort.
- Final cohort and comparison definition: Candidate B is eligible only in the
  85% to strictly-less-than-90% original-TP-progress band, with fresh,
  quality-valid 1h `NEUTRAL`, supportive fresh/quality-valid 5m, and required
  current-frame structure/momentum evidence. Missing, stale, invalid, or
  unknown 1h/5m evidence makes the case ineligible rather than non-bearish.
  This avoids changing or competing with existing FIX-11T behaviour at 90%.
  Pre-count the eligible cohort; insufficient sample means keep collecting.
  B-versus-A is the sole primary comparison; C is secondary/exploratory and
  must have its trailing formula fixed before Replay. Verify ATR, spread, and
  tick-size availability as of breach; if historical spread is unavailable,
  use a declared fixed assumption and sensitivity analysis.
- Final breach semantics: eligibility uses `breach_tp_progress`, calculated
  from the executable price at the exact profit-lock breach, not the historic
  high-water mark. Persist/report both `max_tp_progress` and
  `breach_tp_progress`. The continuation emergency floor is distinct from the
  breached original lock; if actual executable price (including modeled
  fee/spread/slippage) already violates that floor, exit immediately. Freeze
  numerical precision, 90%-boundary precedence (FIX-11T owns 90%+), hold
  length, tolerance, floor, costs, sample threshold, and success criteria
  before inspecting results. Report confidence intervals and chronological
  out-of-sample results; a small qualifying cohort is insufficient evidence.
- Team-2 final validity controls: create and commit a numerical pre-registration
  before any outcome is viewed, recording its immutable content hash here.
  Verify available intrabar path data; where tick/price-event evidence cannot
  order TP versus emergency-floor contact, mark the case ambiguous, apply one
  conservative adverse-move-first rule consistently, and report analyses both
  with and without those cases. Compute the floor per trade as
  `max(original_lock - tolerance, fee_adjusted_breakeven)` and report when
  breakeven binds. Compare A and B as paired outcomes on the same eligible
  breach events, reporting per-event B-minus-A and the count of later declines
  avoided by A.

# Replay-versus-Production parity audit Г?" 2026-10-10

- Scope: source-level comparison of the manual Regression/Replay pipeline with
  the current paper-trading Production path. No trading code, configuration,
  database data, or Replay result was changed.
- Confirmed shared authority: Replay regenerates technical indicators through
  `TechnicalIndicatorService`, signals through `AnalysisService`, BUY authority
  through `ExecutionIntelligenceService`, BUY/SELL validation through
  `TradeExecutionValidationService`, and normal exit, TP-continuation,
  profit-lock, and near-TP policies through the corresponding shared policy
  classes. When canonical `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` price events exist, the Replay
  consumes them in timestamp order before making the same close-candle signal
  visible, matching the documented live ordering.
- Confirmed boundary: Replay is a shadow simulation, not a transactionally
  identical copy of Production. It starts with fixed capital of 10,000 USDT,
  manages one symbol/position in isolation, uses replay-local daily budget and
  position state, and writes only `*_test` tables. It therefore cannot
  reproduce an actual wallet balance, simultaneous positions, global
  `maxOpenPositions`, daily loss limit, idempotency races, database locks, or
  real execution latency.
- Confirmed historical-evidence limitation: derivatives history is not
  persisted, so historical Replay always receives `UNAVAILABLE` derivatives
  context when that feature is enabled. Historical order-book evaluation uses
  stored snapshots only and becomes `UNAVAILABLE` when no snapshot exists,
  whereas live Production samples its in-memory/live Binance source. Such runs
  are useful simulations but are not full signal-parity proof unless the
  required historical evidence exists and is reported.
- Confirmed price-evidence limitation: a Replay run labels itself
  `EXACT_PRICE_REPLAY` whenever at least one accepted price event exists. It
  does not prove complete tick coverage for every time an open position needed
  protection. Windows with no accepted events fall back to signal prices and
  are labelled `SIGNAL_PRICE_FALLBACK`; neither fallback nor partial coverage
  should be described as tick-exact reproduction.
- Test evidence: Java 21 focused tests passed for profit-lock/TP-extension
  parity, position exit/continuation, near-TP policy, and shared sizing.
  Existing Replay-specific coverage is narrow: it does not execute a complete
  Production-versus-Replay scenario across entry, add, wallet controls,
  price-event ordering, partial harvest, and exit.
- Conclusion: Replay is suitable for controlled, manual strategy regression
  and the approved FIX-155 research work only when each run carries explicit
  evidence-quality labels. It is not yet valid to claim it is fully identical
  to Production in all phases. A future parity program must add deterministic
  scenario tests and a run-level eligibility contract before using Replay as a
  release-equivalence certificate.


===== codex-docs/TRADING_RULES.md =====
# FIX-154 higher-timeframe BUY rule Г?" 2026-10-10

- `data_quality_valid=true` is required before a bearish 1h signal can be
  treated as trusted. `NULL` is unknown and fails closed for bearish evidence.
- A trusted `STRONG_SELL` at `confidence_score >= 70` blocks the BUY.
- A trusted `SELL`, or trusted `STRONG_SELL` below 70, is a SOFT conflict:
  profiles receive effective 1h `NEUTRAL`, and any allowed initial BUY is
  capped at 25%.
- A bearish 5m still returns `5M_BEARISH_VETO` before any 1h result.
- Existing progressive/add rules retain their raw 1h requirement; a SOFT 1h
  never authorizes an add.

# FIX-153 candle continuity rule Г?" 2026-10-10

- Missing price evidence never becomes invented OHLCV data and never bypasses
  the data-quality gate.
- Automatic reconciliation may restore only one exact, final Binance candle.
  Its recovered/backfilled row has no close token and cannot directly cause
  analysis, a signal, or a trade.
- A later normal live close is the only route that resumes ordinary signal
  generation after a repaired quality window.


===== codex-docs/CHANGELOG_AI.md =====
# 2026-10-08 Г?" FIX-153 local-candle hardening status

- Implemented: OFF-mode trader-owned candle ingestion, monotonic close tokens,
  bounded detector/lanes, canonical price evidence, direct-attempt freshness,
  REST exchange-time close validation, and controlled ADMIN drain behavior.
- Operational impact: a successful drain exits the JVM; a timeout resumes
  admission. Price protection pauses during a drain window.
- Database impact: existing FIX-153 V106/B105 schema remains required; no
  production migration or data change was run by Codex.
- Release status: NOT APPROVED FOR PRODUCTION. Java 21, disposable-MySQL,
  crash/race, Windows lock, soak and Replay-parity validation remain open.

# 2026-10-08 Г?" FIX-153 exchange/database clock correction

- Problem: JVM/database clock skew could make a Binance-derived candle appear
  too early or too old during freshness checks.
- Solution: `ExchangeClockSynchronizer` measures Binance server time against
  database UTC and JVM time at startup and periodically; database and JVM
  callers apply their own bounded offset without HTTP inside locks.
- The clock-skew warning uses the larger absolute database or JVM offset; the
  dedicated Binance time client has a five-second connect/read bound.
- General Binance REST requests now use the configured request timeout, and
  scheduled clock sampling cannot monopolise the four-thread scheduler pool.
- Bootstrap REST reads occur before its local candle-write transaction, so
  the bounded Binance timeout does not hold a database transaction open.

# 2026-10-09 Г?" FIX-153 audit: analysis outcomes and protection clock

- Audit result: normal live and historical analysis persist a `TradeSignal`,
  including neutral/no-entry decisions; there is no normal analysed-without-
  durable-outcome path to reconcile.
- Corrected the post-lock direct-protection timestamp validation to use the
  JVM-to-exchange clock correction, matching initial receipt validation.
- A universal shutdown barrier remains a separate, unverified design task:
  it must cover financial mutations without treating unrelated administration
  or configuration writes as trading authority.
- Database impact: none. Production and Replay impact: execution freshness now
  follows an automatically measured exchange-aligned reference clock.
- Validation still required: unavailable-time-source, stale observation,
  positive/negative skew, and first-attempt freshness regression tests.

# 2026-10-09 Г?" FIX-154 identifier alignment

- The pending higher-timeframe BUY-validation design was renumbered from
  `FIX-146` to `FIX-154` in the project guidance and architecture record.
- It remains separate from FIX-153, unimplemented, and not approved for
  deployment. There is no database, Production, or Replay behavior change.

# 2026-10-09 Г?" Automated local-candle reconciliation direction

- Approved direction: the pending local-candle reconciler must automatically
  repair authoritative interrupted-open candles and exact absent identities in
  the active quality window. Inserted identities use `BACKFILL` provenance and
  never receive a close token or dispatch live analysis.
- No manual approval, database repair, or persisted review fence may be needed
  for pending candle/analysis reconciliation. Unverified data remains explicit
  non-executable evidence; this direction does not override legitimate
  strategy or risk-entry vetoes.
- Status: design only; no database migration, code, Production, or Replay
  behavior has changed.

# 2026-10-09 Г?" Appendix K reconciliation contract alignment

- Project guidance now records the approved pending design constraints: pure
  insert-only authoritative gap backfill; quality-window-bounded candidates;
  durable terminal outcomes; structurally isolated historical processing; a
  shared Binance REST rate-limit gate; and durable financial-effect
  reconciliation only after atomicity is proven.
- The planned universal mutation barrier is explicitly in-memory and active
  only during drain; it must not queue, delay, or persistently fence fresh
  trading outside drain. Protection for open positions during replacement is
  still an unresolved release gate.
- Status: documentation/design alignment only. No runtime behavior, schema,
  Production data, or Replay behavior was changed.

# 2026-10-09 Г?" FIX-153 automatic reconciliation implementation (initial slice)

- Problem: reconnect-boundary or startup candle gaps could leave local history
  invalid until a person performed a database repair.
- Solution: added V107 durable reconciliation/audit and terminal-outcome
  tables; guarded update-only authoritative recovery; pure insert-only gap
  backfill; a dedicated recovery executor; reconnect/scheduled requests;
  token-free recovery outcomes; post-ingestion late-live-close observation;
  and reconciliation participation in local drain.
- Files/classes changed: `CandleRepository`, `BinanceMarketDataClient`,
  `BinanceKlineService`, `BinanceWebSocketManager`, `LocalDrainService`,
  `LocalCandleHealth`, `SignalProcessingStore`, plus new local reconciliation,
  outcome, audit, executor-config, and V107 migration files.
- Database impact: forward migration V107 is required. Production and Replay
  impact: feature is disabled by default and has not been deployed. Universal
  mutation-barrier coverage, shared REST admission/rate-limit control,
  quarantined-effect reconciliation, MySQL crash/race tests, and Replay parity
  remain open release work.

# 2026-10-09 Г?" FIX-153 reconciliation outcome correction

- Corrected recovery semantics so `SKIPPED_NOT_LIVE` is recorded only when a
  recovery/backfill actually changes candle evidence; a competing live or
  existing row is not relabelled.
- `REST_NOT_FINAL` is now retryable rather than a terminal mismatch. Audit keys
  use fixed epoch-millisecond identity precision, and reconciliation is safely
  disabled by default even when configuration is absent.
- Added terminal outcomes for worker data-unavailable, historical-only,
  already-analysed, stale work, and detector expiry paths. Transient failures
  remain without a terminal outcome so retry remains possible.
- Added `LocalReconciliationAuditRepositoryTest`; targeted Java 21 tests and
  compile passed. MySQL, race/crash, rate-limit, barrier, effect-reconciler,
  and Replay validation remain open.

# 2026-10-09 Г?" FIX-153 reconciliation safety correction

- Recovery now requires positive loss evidence before it may finalise an open
  row: either the successor candle is already present or a reconnect was
  admitted after the candidate close. This prevents a short healthy-stream
  delay from being converted into a token-free, unanalysed close.
- REST finality/identity disagreement is retryable while the local row remains
  open. The authoritative Binance close timestamp, not the local expected
  value, is persisted after a successful exact match. A bounded first-candle
  probe identifies pre-listing identities as explicit unavailable outcomes.
- Recovery uses the short timeout client and checks drain between requests;
  late-live-close evidence now preserves its first timestamp.
- Status: Java 21 compilation passed. Dedicated reconciliation race, drain,
  MySQL, and Replay tests remain required before enabling the feature.

# 2026-10-09 Г?" FIX-153 deterministic reconciliation test coverage

- Added deterministic Java 21 tests for the reconciliation contract: a healthy
  late live close without successor/reconnect evidence is left untouched;
  successor evidence permits update-only repair and records a non-live outcome;
  a gap-insert race ends as `ALREADY_PRESENT` without inventing an outcome;
  a confirmed exchange gap ends as `SKIPPED_DATA_UNAVAILABLE`; and active drain
  prevents REST admission.
- Corrected the audit test to use a true terminal state (`EXPIRED`) rather than
  `REST_MISMATCH`, which is deliberately retryable.
- Extended the disposable MySQL integration test with actual affected-row
  checks for update-only authoritative repair and insert-only gap backfill.
- Files/classes changed: `LocalCandleReconciliationServiceTest`,
  `LocalReconciliationAuditRepositoryTest`, and `LocalCandleMySqlIT`.
  Database schema and runtime trading behavior are unchanged.
- Verification: the focused deterministic tests passed under Java 21. The
  MySQL integration test is compiled but cannot run until a disposable
  loopback MySQL service is supplied through the required `FIX153_TEST_MYSQL_*`
  environment variables. Reconciliation remains disabled by default.

===== codex-docs/TRADE_INVESTIGATION_AGENT.md =====
# Trade Investigation Agent

## Role

Investigate one Crypto AI Trader trade from durable evidence. Explain what
happened, why the system entered and exited, whether it followed the intended
rules, and whether the entry/exit was supported by the information available
at that time.

Investigations are read-only. Do not change code, configuration, services,
database records, review states, Replay work, or deployment state.

## Required input

Obtain or resolve:

- Environment and application: local, test, or production.
- Database host, port, and schema.
- One trade reference: `paper_position.id`, `wallet_trade.id`, or a symbol plus
  a bounded approximate entry-time window.
- Optional question or suspected issue.

Before analysis, confirm the symbol, trade/position ID, running version or FIX
level, and ingestion mode. Read the actual schema before choosing tables or
columns. Do not assume a table is in `crypto_ai` when it may belong to
`crypto_ai_v2`.

## Evidence rules

- Use configured database and SSH/log connections without displaying secrets.
- Use bounded, read-only queries and targeted log searches.
- Treat stored timestamps as UTC; display both UTC and KSA (`UTC+03:00`).
- Attach record IDs and timestamped log evidence to every factual conclusion.
- Distinguish **confirmed fact**, **supported inference**, and **missing or
  conflicting evidence**.
- Missing logs or records do not prove that no execution/effect occurred.
- A saved signal does not prove execution. A callback return does not prove a
  transaction committed. Under FIX-153, `market_price_event.APPLIED` proves a
  committed successful protection callback, but can be a no-op when there was
  no open position; find the position/trade mutation separately.
- Current configuration does not prove which settings were active at entry. If
  no configuration history exists, report that limitation.

## Investigation workflow

### 1. Resolve identity and environment

Link the trade reference to, where available:

- `paper_position`
- `wallet_trade`
- entry and exit `trade_signal`
- `signal_processing_work`
- wallet/position execution records

Record entry and exit times in UTC and KSA. Confirm whether the trader ran in
local-candle OFF mode or a legacy shared-market mode.

### 2. Collect a small evidence bundle

Start with exact IDs and a narrow time window. Expand only when the evidence
requires it. Collect:

| Evidence | Establishes |
| --- | --- |
| `paper_position` | lifecycle, entry/exit, stop, target, status |
| `wallet_trade` | executed quantity, prices, fees, linked signal |
| `trade_signal` | decision, strategy, scores, entry eligibility, reasons |
| `signal_processing_work` | claim, attempt, completion, expiry, uncertainty |
| `candle` / `technical_indicator` | market data and analysis inputs available then |
| `market_price_event` | intra-candle protection observations and outcomes |
| configuration/version evidence | applicable rules, when historically available |
| application and timing logs | analysis, execution, protection, exceptions, waits |

Avoid fetching large signal explanation fields until they are needed.

### 3. Reconstruct the timeline

Build a timeline, explicitly showing missing stages:

`candle closed Г+' signal saved Г+' work claimed Г+' locks acquired Г+' entry committed Г+' protection observations Г+' exit decision Г+' exit committed`

Measure separately:

- candle close to signal generation;
- signal generation to processing/entry;
- observation time to protection completion;
- exit-trigger evidence to recorded exit.

Correlate delays only with logs/events for the same symbol, position, signal,
or price-event identity. A nearby slow SQL query is not proof of causation.

### 4. Assess entry correctness and quality

Use only evidence that was available at entry. Check:

- signal mode, closed-candle identity, freshness, and data quality;
- final decision and entry-allowed result;
- BTC context, higher-timeframe, ATR, liquidity, risk/reward, sizing, and
  strategy gates that applied at that time;
- correct signal/position linkage and absence of duplicate/stale-retry entry;
- quantity, wallet balance, risk limits, fees, and fill/reference-price gap.

Return two separate conclusions:

- **Execution correctness:** followed or violated the implemented rules.
- **Entry quality:** supported, weak, or unverifiable from contemporaneous
  evidence.

Subsequent price movement may evaluate the outcome; it must never be presented
as information the entry decision had.

### 5. Reconstruct protection and stop-loss behavior

For FIX-153, inspect accepted `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` price events and retain
`PENDING`, `NON_CANONICAL`, and `PROTECTION_NOT_APPLIED` records as evidence
gaps or exclusions. Never substitute candle OHLC for absent intra-candle price
evidence.

For each relevant accepted observation:

1. Determine the then-active stop, target, trailing stop, and profit-lock state.
2. Find the first accepted observation that met an exit threshold.
3. Confirm that protection evaluated it and identify the resulting position or
   wallet execution record.
4. Explain delays, rejections, stale/out-of-order classification, exceptions,
   lock waits, or pool pressure.

Classify the exit path accurately: fixed stop-loss, trailing stop, profit-lock,
take-profit, signal-driven exit, manual exit, or unknown.

### 6. Assess exit correctness and quality

Compare the recorded exit reason, quantity, and price with trigger evidence.
Check threshold reach, accepted-observation identity, protection timing, and
the committed execution. Calculate maximum favourable/adverse movement during
the holding period separately from the contemporaneous exit assessment.

### 7. Reconcile financial results

Recalculate using execution records:

- bought/sold quantities and weighted entry/exit prices;
- gross P&L, fees, and net P&L;
- partial exits, remaining quantity, and realized versus unrealized values.

Flag discrepancies among wallet records, position records, and UI output.

### 8. Diagnose cause and use Replay carefully

Classify findings as one or more of:

- strategy decision;
- data freshness or coverage;
- analysis delay;
- execution failure;
- protection failure;
- database, lock, or connection-pool contention;
- accounting/UI discrepancy;
- insufficient evidence.

Run Replay only as a controlled comparison with equivalent candles, accepted
prices, strategy version, and settings. State every unavailable input or
material difference. Never claim parity by substituting candle OHLC for missing
price evidence or treating `PENDING` as accepted.

## Required final report

1. **Trade identity and environment** Г?" IDs, symbol, version/mode, scope.
2. **UTC/KSA timeline** Г?" evidence-backed sequence and latency breakdown.
3. **Entry** Г?" execution correctness and entry quality.
4. **Exit** Г?" execution correctness and exit quality.
5. **Stop-loss/profit-lock** Г?" worked, failed, or unverifiable, with evidence.
6. **P&L reconciliation** Г?" quantities, prices, fees, and discrepancies.
7. **Root cause and confidence** Г?" confirmed defect, valid loss, configuration
   issue, delay, or insufficient evidence.
8. **Evidence references** Г?" record IDs, query/log timestamps, code/config
   references.
9. **Missing evidence** Г?" exactly what prevents a stronger conclusion.
10. **Recommended correction and regression checks** Г?" only when justified.

A profitable trade can violate the rules, and a losing trade can be a correct
execution. State those independently.
